Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi. Well, I came in because... things at work just took a really weird turn. I'm a production manager at a manufacturing plant, and I went from launching these nonstop plant redesign projects—which, by the way, the floor layout has been a total disaster since they installed the new conveyor belts last year. I actually wrote my graduate thesis on industrial ergonomics, so it drives me absolutely crazy when people don't optimize their workspace. My professor used to say that bad routing costs millions, and he was right. 

But anyway, I took it upon myself to fix everything. I was calling our suppliers after hours. I remember Gary, our main plastics vendor, got so huffy when I called him at midnight on a Tuesday, but I was just so dialed in and needed the specs right then. I even rewrote the entire floor shift plan completely by myself. 

But... then... I don't know. Suddenly I just hit this massive wall. I became completely unable to keep up with the very plans I had just set in motion. Like, I couldn't even look at the schedules I just made. I just couldn't do it anymore.

Yeah, I mean, at first I felt almost... electrically upbeat. Like I was plugged right into the main power grid of the plant. But then it shifted and I got just weirdly irritable. It was like... if anyone even questioned my ideas, they were just slowing down something that was so obvious and important. Which reminds me, my sister does that all the time. She'll ask me these completely pointless questions when I'm trying to explain something simple, like how to properly load the dishwasher. I swear, she puts the bowls in the bottom rack facing up, and it just collects that dirty, soapy water. I've told her a million times that the spray arm needs to reach the inside, but she just rolls her eyes. She actually bought this new detergent that smells like lavender, which is supposed to be calming, right? But honestly, the smell just gives me a headache. I read somewhere that synthetic fragrances can actually disrupt your endocrine system. I should probably switch to those unscented pods... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the plant projects. Yeah, I just couldn't stand anyone getting in my way.

Well, this heavy, completely drained stretch... it's been going on for about three months now. It just hit me so hard. And honestly, it's been so much worse because of the recent move and getting handed that massive plant assignment right when I got here. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up my entire apartment, and I swear, I have way too many books. I kept telling myself I was going to donate half of them, but then you start looking at them and thinking, "Oh, I might read this biography of Henry Ford again." Which, by the way, he was a terrible person, but his assembly line concepts were revolutionary. Did you know he actually paid his workers five dollars a day, which was huge back then? He did it so they could afford the cars they were building. It makes total sense economically. But anyway, the movers scratched my vintage credenza, and I spent like three hours trying to buff it out with this special wax I found online. The shipping took forever because it came from this tiny shop in Maine. I've always wanted to go to Maine, especially in the fall for the foliage. But yeah... three months of just feeling like I can't even look at a schedule or care about any of it.

I had to pack up my entire apartment, and I swear, I have way too many books. I kept telling myself I was going to donate half of them, but then you start looking at them and thinking, \"Oh, I might read this biography of Henry Ford again.\" Which, by the way, he was a terrible person, but his assembly line concepts were revolutionary. But anyway, the movers scratched my vintage credenza, and I spent like three hours trying to buff it out with this special wax I found online. The shipping took forever because it came from this tiny shop in Maine. I've always wanted to go to Maine, especially in the fall for the foliage. Oh my gosh, they make these incredible almond croissants. Yeah, it's... it's definitely worse when I first open my eyes. Lately I wake up in that temporary apartment and just have this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day is already completely ruined before I even get in my car to drive to the plant. And the drive to the plant is awful anyway. The traffic on I-95 is just a nightmare, especially near that one exit where they've been doing construction for what feels like a decade. I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. My dad used to work in civil engineering, and he always complained about how city contracts are managed. He actually retired early and bought this RV. He and my mom just drive around the country now, going to all these national parks. They send me postcards, which is sweet, but I never know where to put them. I bought a corkboard to pin them up, but the pushpins keep falling out because the cork is too thin. It was one of those cheap ones from the dollar store. I really should invest in a nice linen bulletin board. But yeah... mornings are just really, really hard. It feels impossible to even get out of bed.

No, I wouldn't say I'm worried or scared about the future. I'm just... I just don't care about it right now. It's too much effort to even think about tomorrow, let alone next week. Although, I did see on the news that they're predicting a really bad hurricane season this year. The ocean temperatures are apparently way higher than average, which fuels the storms. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. We ended up playing Monopoly by candlelight, which sounds fun, but Monopoly actually takes forever and always ends in an argument. My roommate got so mad she flipped the board, and we spent an hour looking for the little silver shoe piece in the dark. I always played as the dog, anyway. Dogs are just better, you know? I really want to get a golden retriever someday, but my apartment complex has a strict no-pets policy. They don't even allow fish tanks over ten gallons because of "water damage liability," which seems a little extreme. But no, no excessive worry. Just this massive weight of not having the energy to deal with any of it.

Oh my gosh, they make these incredible almond croissants. The baker actually studied in Paris, and you can totally tell because the pastry is so flaky, not like that dense stuff you get at the grocery store. I tried making croissants at home once during lockdown, and it was a total disaster. The butter has to be the exact right temperature, and my kitchen gets way too warm in the afternoon because it faces west. I really need to get better blinds for those windows. The sun just beats in and fades the rug. I read that UV-blocking window film is pretty easy to install yourself, but... I don't know. I just don't have the energy for any of that right now. I don't really have the energy for anything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significantly depressed mood that is worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's definitely worse when I first open my eyes. Lately I wake up in that temporary apartment and just have this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day is already completely ruined before I even get in my car to drive to the plant. I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. My dad used to work in civil engineering, and he always complained about how city contracts are managed. He actually retired early and bought this RV. He and my mom just drive around the country now, going to all these national parks. They send me postcards, which is sweet, but I never know where to put them. I bought a corkboard to pin them up, but the pushpins keep falling out because the cork is too thin. It was one of those cheap ones from the dollar store. I really should invest in a nice linen bulletin board. But yeah... mornings are just really, really hard. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. I had to pack up my entire apartment, and I swear, I have way too many books. But anyway, the movers scratched my vintage credenza, and I spent like three hours trying to buff it out with this special wax I found online. The shipping took forever because it came from this tiny shop in Maine. I've always wanted to go to Maine, especially in the fall for the foliage. But yeah... three months of just feeling like I can't even look at a schedule or care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities or hobbies that you used to find pleasurable before you started feeling drained?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, I used to love hiking on the weekends. I had this whole route mapped out at the state park. But lately, just the thought of putting on my hiking boots feels like climbing a mountain itself. Which is a shame because those boots were so expensive. I bought them at this specialty outdoor store downtown, the one next to that amazing little bakery? Oh my gosh, they make these incredible almond croissants. The baker actually studied in Paris, and you can totally tell because the pastry is so flaky, not like that dense stuff you get at the grocery store. I tried making croissants at home once during lockdown, and it was a total disaster. The butter has to be the exact right temperature, and my kitchen gets way too warm in the afternoon because it faces west. I really need to get better blinds for those windows. The sun just beats in and fades the rug. I read that UV-blocking window film is pretty easy to install yourself, but... I don't know. I just don't have the energy for any of that right now. I don't really have the energy for anything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significantly depressed mood that is worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's definitely worse when I first open my eyes. Lately I wake up in that temporary apartment and just have this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day is already completely ruined before I even get in my car to drive to the plant. And the drive to the plant is awful anyway. The traffic on I-95 is just a nightmare, especially near that one exit where they've been doing construction for what feels like a decade. I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. My dad used to work in civil engineering, and he always complained about how city contracts are managed. He actually retired early and bought this RV. He and my mom just drive around the country now, going to all these national parks. They send me postcards, which is sweet, but I never know where to put them. I bought a corkboard to pin them up, but the pushpins keep falling out because the cork is too thin. It was one of those cheap ones from the dollar store. I really should invest in a nice linen bulletin board. But yeah... mornings are just really, really hard. It feels impossible to even get out of bed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm worried or scared about the future. I'm just... I just don't care about it right now. It's too much effort to even think about tomorrow, let alone next week. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. We ended up playing Monopoly by candlelight, which sounds fun, but Monopoly actually takes forever and always ends in an argument. My roommate got so mad she flipped the board, and we spent an hour looking for the little silver shoe piece in the dark. I always played as the dog, anyway. Dogs are just better, you know? I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. But then I got really, weirdly irritable because the floor supervisors kept questioning my plans, and it felt like they were just slowing down something that was so obviously important and brilliant. Which is exactly what happened when I tried to reorganize my kitchen cabinets last year. I had this whole system figured out based on frequency of use and ergonomic reach, but my sister came over and said it didn't make sense to put the coffee mugs on the bottom shelf. But she doesn't even drink coffee! She's one of those people who drinks matcha lattes every morning. She buys this ceremonial grade powder online that costs like forty dollars a tin. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. But yeah, at the beginning of the assignment, I just felt super charged up and then really annoyed with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric that lasted for at least several days?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this intense shift from being in total overdrive to barely functioning at all, it's been going on since I moved into that temporary apartment. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. It's one of those old electric coils that sits crooked, so the pan slides to one side and all the oil pools at the edge. It makes cooking chicken practically impossible because one side burns and the other is raw. But anyway, yeah, this whole up and down thing has been happening for about three weeks.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had other periods like this in the past where you felt extremely upbeat and then crashed, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / industrial_production_manager / graduate\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't really know, honestly. I mean, I've had times where I felt pretty good and then kind of tired, but I don't know if it's like this. It's hard to remember, especially because my memory has felt so fuzzy lately. It's like trying to remember a dream right after you wake up, you know? You have the feeling of it, but the details just slip away. I had this dream the other night that I was trying to run on a treadmill, but the belt was made of quicksand, and I just kept sinking. I woke up sweating, and it was freezing in that apartment because the windows are super drafty. I bought this plastic film stuff you're supposed to shrink-wrap over the windows with a hair dryer to keep the cold out, but my hair dryer broke. It just sparked and died right in the middle of drying my hair before work last Tuesday. I had to go in with half-wet hair, and it was so embarrassing. My hair always frizzes up when it air dries. I used to use this special anti-frizz serum, but they discontinued it. Why do companies always discontinue the best products? It's like they want us to be unhappy. So, I don't know if I've had periods exactly like this before. It's just hard to say.

Well, like I was saying, I really can't say for sure. It's just hard to pin down if I've had these exact same shifts before. I mean, my mood definitely fluctuates, but who doesn't have good days and bad days? Like I just said, I... I really don't know. I can't remember another time when I felt like that for days on end. It's just so hard to think back clearly when my head feels like it's stuffed with cotton right now. Cotton always makes me think of those cheap pillows they give you in hotels. You know the ones? They look all fluffy until you lay your head on them, and then they just flatten into a pancake. I travel a lot for work, inspecting different production facilities, so I've slept on my fair share of terrible hotel pillows. There was this one place in Ohio, right near the stamping plant, where the pillow was actually lumpy. Like, literal chunks of foam inside. I tried folding it in half, but that just made it worse. The plant manager there was this guy named Gary who collected vintage lunchboxes. He had a whole wall of them in his office. A Six Million Dollar Man one, a Scooby-Doo one... it was actually kind of neat, but he talked about them for an hour while we were supposed to be reviewing safety protocols. Safety is a huge deal on the production floor. You have to wear steel-toed boots, which are so heavy. But yeah, I don't know if I've had another time where I was super energetic like that. I just can't say for sure.

I don't really have the energy for anything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significantly depressed mood that is worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's definitely worse when I first open my eyes. Lately I wake up in that temporary apartment and just have this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day is already completely ruined before I even get in my car to drive to the plant. And the drive to the plant is awful anyway. The traffic on I-95 is just a nightmare, especially near that one exit where they've been doing construction for what feels like a decade. I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. My dad used to work in civil engineering, and he always complained about how city contracts are managed. He actually retired early and bought this RV. He and my mom just drive around the country now, going to all these national parks. They send me postcards, which is sweet, but I never know where to put them. I bought a corkboard to pin them up, but the pushpins keep falling out because the cork is too thin. It was one of those cheap ones from the dollar store. I really should invest in a nice linen bulletin board. But yeah... mornings are just really, really hard. It feels impossible to even get out of bed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm worried or scared about the future. I'm just... I just don't care about it right now. It's too much effort to even think about tomorrow, let alone next week. Although, I did see on the news that they're predicting a really bad hurricane season this year. The ocean temperatures are apparently way higher than average, which fuels the storms. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. We ended up playing Monopoly by candlelight, which sounds fun, but Monopoly actually takes forever and always ends in an argument. My roommate got so mad she flipped the board, and we spent an hour looking for the little silver shoe piece in the dark. I always played as the dog, anyway. Dogs are just better, you know? I really want to get a golden retriever someday, but my apartment complex has a strict no-pets policy. They don't even allow fish tanks over ten gallons because of \"water damage liability,\" which seems a little extreme. But no, no excessive worry. Just this massive weight of not having the energy to deal with any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric for a long time?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. When I first started this new assignment at the plant, I felt... I don't know, almost electrically upbeat? Like I was just vibrating with all these incredible ideas for streamlining the production line. But then I got really, weirdly irritable because the floor supervisors kept questioning my plans, and it felt like they were just slowing down something that was so obviously important and brilliant. Which is exactly what happened when I tried to reorganize my kitchen cabinets last year. I had this whole system figured out based on frequency of use and ergonomic reach, but my sister came over and said it didn't make sense to put the coffee mugs on the bottom shelf. But she doesn't even drink coffee! She's one of those people who drinks matcha lattes every morning. She buys this ceremonial grade powder online that costs like forty dollars a tin. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. But yeah, at the beginning of the assignment, I just felt super charged up and then really annoyed with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric that lasted for at least several days?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this intense shift from being in total overdrive to barely functioning at all, it's been going on since I moved into that temporary apartment. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I really just don't have a better answer for you. I haven't kept a log of my moods or anything, so I honestly don't know if there's been a pattern of going up and then crashing. It's all just a blur right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Like I just said, I... I really don't know. I can't remember another time when I felt like that for days on end. It's just so hard to think back clearly when my head feels like it's stuffed with cotton right now. Cotton always makes me think of those cheap pillows they give you in hotels. You know the ones? They look all fluffy until you lay your head on them, and then they just flatten into a pancake. I travel a lot for work, inspecting different production facilities, so I've slept on my fair share of terrible hotel pillows. There was this one place in Ohio, right near the stamping plant, where the pillow was actually lumpy. Like, literal chunks of foam inside. I tried folding it in half, but that just made it worse. The plant manager there was this guy named Gary who collected vintage lunchboxes. He had a whole wall of them in his office. A Six Million Dollar Man one, a Scooby-Doo one... it was actually kind of neat, but he talked about them for an hour while we were supposed to be reviewing safety protocols. Safety is a huge deal on the production floor. You have to wear steel-toed boots, which are so heavy. But yeah, I don't know if I've had another time where I was super energetic like that. I just can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic, followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I really just don't have a better answer for you. I haven't kept a log of my moods or anything, so I honestly don't know if there's been a pattern of going up and then crashing. It's kind of like asking me to pull a specific quality assurance report from, I don't know, three years ago. Without a tracking number, it's just lost in the void. We actually used to use these massive, heavy physical binders for QA on the assembly line before we finally switched to digital tablets last quarter. The tablets are supposed to be so much more efficient, but the glare on the screens under those harsh fluorescent factory lights is absolutely terrible. I had to order these special matte anti-glare screen protectors, but the purchasing department held up the order for three weeks because of some ridiculous budget freeze. Purchasing departments always ruin everything, I swear. They'll pinch pennies on a fifty-dollar pack of screen protectors, but then they turn around and approve a ten-thousand-dollar catering budget for the regional executive retreat. It makes absolutely no sense, the way corporate allocates funds. But anyway, yeah, I haven't noticed a specific timeline of episodes like what you're asking about. I just really couldn't tell you.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / industrial_production_manager / graduate\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"At the beginning of this assignment I felt almost electrically upbeat and then weirdly irritable, like anyone questioning my ideas was slowing down something obvious and important.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I know you keep asking that, and I guess... I mean, at the very beginning of this new assignment, I did feel almost electrically upbeat for a while. Like I was totally dialed in. But then it turned into me getting weirdly irritable. It just felt like anyone who was questioning my ideas was slowing down something that was so obvious and important, you know? Like Greg from engineering. He always has to play devil's advocate on the floor. And he wears these awful argyle sweater vests, even in the summer. Who wears wool in July? The AC in the main office isn't even that great anyway because the HVAC system hasn't been updated since 1998. They keep saying they're going to overhaul it, but then they just bring in these cheap little oscillating fans that just push the hot air around the room. It dries out my contacts so badly. I actually had to switch to dailies because my eyes were getting so red and dry. Dailies are so ridiculously expensive though, and my vision insurance only covers a fraction of the cost. Vision insurance is basically a scam if you ask me. But yeah, I was definitely feeling really upbeat and then just so annoyed with everyone at the start of this project.

I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. He and my mom just drive around the country now, going to all these national parks. They send me postcards, which is sweet, but I never know where to put them. I bought a corkboard to pin them up, but the pushpins keep falling out because the cork is too thin. It was one of those cheap ones from the dollar store. I really should invest in a nice linen bulletin board. But yeah... mornings are just really, really hard. It feels impossible to even get out of bed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm worried or scared about the future. I'm just... I just don't care about it right now. It's too much effort to even think about tomorrow, let alone next week. Although, I did see on the news that they're predicting a really bad hurricane season this year. The ocean temperatures are apparently way higher than average, which fuels the storms. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. We ended up playing Monopoly by candlelight, which sounds fun, but Monopoly actually takes forever and always ends in an argument. My roommate got so mad she flipped the board, and we spent an hour looking for the little silver shoe piece in the dark. I always played as the dog, anyway. Dogs are just better, you know? They don't even allow fish tanks over ten gallons because of \"water damage liability,\" which seems a little extreme. But no, no excessive worry. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. But yeah, at the beginning of the assignment, I just felt super charged up and then really annoyed with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric that lasted for at least several days?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this intense shift from being in total overdrive to barely functioning at all, it's been going on since I moved into that temporary apartment. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. It's one of those old electric coils that sits crooked, so the pan slides to one side and all the oil pools at the edge. It makes cooking chicken practically impossible because one side burns and the other is raw. But anyway, yeah, this whole up and down thing has been happening for about three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods like this in the past where you felt extremely upbeat and then crashed, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I mean, I've had times where I felt pretty good and then kind of tired, but I don't know if it's like this. It's hard to remember, especially because my memory has felt so fuzzy lately. It's like trying to remember a dream right after you wake up, you know? You have the feeling of it, but the details just slip away. I had this dream the other night that I was trying to run on a treadmill, but the belt was made of quicksand, and I just kept sinking. I woke up sweating, and it was freezing in that apartment because the windows are super drafty. I bought this plastic film stuff you're supposed to shrink-wrap over the windows with a hair dryer to keep the cold out, but my hair dryer broke. It just sparked and died right in the middle of drying my hair before work last Tuesday. I had to go in with half-wet hair, and it was so embarrassing. My hair always frizzes up when it air dries. I used to use this special anti-frizz serum, but they discontinued it. Why do companies always discontinue the best products? It's like they want us to be unhappy. When I first started this new assignment at the plant, I felt... I don't know, almost electrically upbeat? Like I was just vibrating with all these incredible ideas for streamlining the production line. But then I got really, weirdly irritable because the floor supervisors kept questioning my plans, and it felt like they were just slowing down something that was so obviously important and brilliant. Which is exactly what happened when I tried to reorganize my kitchen cabinets last year. I had this whole system figured out based on frequency of use and ergonomic reach, but my sister came over and said it didn't make sense to put the coffee mugs on the bottom shelf. But she doesn't even drink coffee! She's one of those people who drinks matcha lattes every morning. She buys this ceremonial grade powder online that costs like forty dollars a tin. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. But yeah, at the beginning of the assignment, I just felt super charged up and then really annoyed with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric that lasted for at least several days?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this intense shift from being in total overdrive to barely functioning at all, it's been going on since I moved into that temporary apartment. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. It's one of those old electric coils that sits crooked, so the pan slides to one side and all the oil pools at the edge. It makes cooking chicken practically impossible because one side burns and the other is raw. But anyway, yeah, this whole up and down thing has been happening for about three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods like this in the past where you felt extremely upbeat and then crashed, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I mean, I've had times where I felt pretty good and then kind of tired, but I don't know if it's like this. It's hard to remember, especially because my memory has felt so fuzzy lately. It's like trying to remember a dream right after you wake up, you know? You have the feeling of it, but the details just slip away. I had this dream the other night that I was trying to run on a treadmill, but the belt was made of quicksand, and I just kept sinking. I woke up sweating, and it was freezing in that apartment because the windows are super drafty. I bought this plastic film stuff you're supposed to shrink-wrap over the windows with a hair dryer to keep the cold out, but my hair dryer broke. It just sparked and died right in the middle of drying my hair before work last Tuesday. I had to go in with half-wet hair, and it was so embarrassing. My hair always frizzes up when it air dries. I used to use this special anti-frizz serum, but they discontinued it. Why do companies always discontinue the best products? It's like they want us to be unhappy. So, I don't know if I've had periods exactly like this before. It's just hard to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these extreme mood shifts from being very upbeat to feeling completely drained before in your life?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I really can't say for sure. It's just hard to pin down if I've had these exact same shifts before. I mean, my mood definitely fluctuates, but who doesn't have good days and bad days? It's just that right now everything feels so magnified, probably because of the stress of the new assignment and the move. Moving is just so disruptive to your routine. I usually go to this spin class on Tuesdays and Thursdays, but the studio is all the way across town now. The instructor, Dave, plays the best 80s rock playlists. I tried to find a new studio closer to the temporary apartment, but they all play that awful EDM music with the heavy bass that gives me a headache. I get migraines sometimes, especially when the barometric pressure drops. My mom gets them too. She swears by peppermint oil rubbed on her temples, but I think it just makes you smell like a candy cane. Candy canes always remind me of when I was a kid and we'd decorate the tree on Christmas Eve. We had these vintage glass ornaments that were so fragile. Anyway, no, I just really don't know if I've had these extreme shifts like this before. It's all just a blur right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Like I just said, I... I really don't know. I can't remember another time when I felt like that for days on end. It's just so hard to think back clearly when my head feels like it's stuffed with cotton right now. Cotton always makes me think of those cheap pillows they give you in hotels. You know the ones? They look all fluffy until you lay your head on them, and then they just flatten into a pancake. I travel a lot for work, inspecting different production facilities, so I've slept on my fair share of terrible hotel pillows. There was this one place in Ohio, right near the stamping plant, where the pillow was actually lumpy. Like, literal chunks of foam inside. I tried folding it in half, but that just made it worse. The plant manager there was this guy named Gary who collected vintage lunchboxes. He had a whole wall of them in his office. A Six Million Dollar Man one, a Scooby-Doo one... it was actually kind of neat, but he talked about them for an hour while we were supposed to be reviewing safety protocols. Safety is a huge deal on the production floor. You have to wear steel-toed boots, which are so heavy. But yeah, I don't know if I've had another time where I was super energetic like that. I just can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic, followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I really just don't have a better answer for you. I haven't kept a log of my moods or anything, so I honestly don't know if there's been a pattern of going up and then crashing. It's kind of like asking me to pull a specific quality assurance report from, I don't know, three years ago. Without a tracking number, it's just lost in the void. We actually used to use these massive, heavy physical binders for QA on the assembly line before we finally switched to digital tablets last quarter. The tablets are supposed to be so much more efficient, but the glare on the screens under those harsh fluorescent factory lights is absolutely terrible. I had to order these special matte anti-glare screen protectors, but the purchasing department held up the order for three weeks because of some ridiculous budget freeze. Purchasing departments always ruin everything, I swear. They'll pinch pennies on a fifty-dollar pack of screen protectors, but then they turn around and approve a ten-thousand-dollar catering budget for the regional executive retreat. It makes absolutely no sense, the way corporate allocates funds. But anyway, yeah, I haven't noticed a specific timeline of episodes like what you're asking about. I just really couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, I know you keep asking that, and I guess... Well, before this plant assignment... I guess there was a time, yeah. For a stretch, I had this massive surplus of energy. It was like I didn't even need to sleep. I remember I unpacked all my boxes after midnight, completely reorganized my apartment, answered a mountain of work emails, and I still wanted to drive back to the plant to check on one more thing on the line. Driving at night is actually really peaceful, though. There's hardly anyone on the road, especially on that stretch of highway near my place. They've been doing construction on it for what feels like three years. They always leave those bright orange barrels out, even when no one is working. I don't understand why they can't just move them to the shoulder. It creates this artificial bottleneck that backs up traffic for miles during rush hour. And people are so aggressive when they have to merge. This one guy in a silver pickup truck almost ran me off the road last week because he refused to zipper merge properly. Zipper merging is the most efficient way to handle a lane closure, it's mathematically proven, but people just get so territorial about their spot in line. It's like they think they're losing some sort of race. But yeah, I definitely had that period where I was just going and going, unpacking and working all night.

I've told her a million times that the spray arm needs to reach the inside, but she just rolls her eyes. She actually bought this new detergent that smells like lavender, which is supposed to be calming, right? But honestly, the smell just gives me a headache. I read somewhere that synthetic fragrances can actually disrupt your endocrine system. I should probably switch to those unscented pods... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the plant projects. Yeah, I just couldn't stand anyone getting in my way.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt unable to keep up with your plans and lost interest in the things you were working on?\n\nPatient: Well, this heavy, completely drained stretch... it's been going on for about three months now. It just hit me so hard. And honestly, it's been so much worse because of the recent move and getting handed that massive plant assignment right when I got here. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up my entire apartment, and I swear, I have way too many books. I kept telling myself I was going to donate half of them, but then you start looking at them and thinking, \"Oh, I might read this biography of Henry Ford again.\" Which, by the way, he was a terrible person, but his assembly line concepts were revolutionary. Did you know he actually paid his workers five dollars a day, which was huge back then? He did it so they could afford the cars they were building. It makes total sense economically. But anyway, the movers scratched my vintage credenza, and I spent like three hours trying to buff it out with this special wax I found online. The shipping took forever because it came from this tiny shop in Maine. I've always wanted to go to Maine, especially in the fall for the foliage. But yeah... three months of just feeling like I can't even look at a schedule or care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities or hobbies that you used to find pleasurable before you started feeling drained?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, I used to love hiking on the weekends. I had this whole route mapped out at the state park. But lately, just the thought of putting on my hiking boots feels like climbing a mountain itself. Which is a shame because those boots were so expensive. I bought them at this specialty outdoor store downtown, the one next to that amazing little bakery? Oh my gosh, they make these incredible almond croissants. The baker actually studied in Paris, and you can totally tell because the pastry is so flaky, not like that dense stuff you get at the grocery store. I tried making croissants at home once during lockdown, and it was a total disaster. The butter has to be the exact right temperature, and my kitchen gets way too warm in the afternoon because it faces west. I really need to get better blinds for those windows. The sun just beats in and fades the rug. I read that UV-blocking window film is pretty easy to install yourself, but... I don't know. I just don't have the energy for any of that right now. I don't really have the energy for anything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significantly depressed mood that is worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's definitely worse when I first open my eyes. Lately I wake up in that temporary apartment and just have this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day is already completely ruined before I even get in my car to drive to the plant. And the drive to the plant is awful anyway. The traffic on I-95 is just a nightmare, especially near that one exit where they've been doing construction for what feels like a decade. I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. My dad used to work in civil engineering, and he always complained about how city contracts are managed. He actually retired early and bought this RV. He and my mom just drive around the country now, going to all these national parks. They send me postcards, which is sweet, but I never know where to put them. I bought a corkboard to pin them up, but the pushpins keep falling out because the cork is too thin. It was one of those cheap ones from the dollar store. I really should invest in a nice linen bulletin board. But yeah... mornings are just really, really hard. It feels impossible to even get out of bed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm worried or scared about the future. I'm just... I just don't care about it right now. It's too much effort to even think about tomorrow, let alone next week. Although, I did see on the news that they're predicting a really bad hurricane season this year. The ocean temperatures are apparently way higher than average, which fuels the storms. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. We ended up playing Monopoly by candlelight, which sounds fun, but Monopoly actually takes forever and always ends in an argument. My roommate got so mad she flipped the board, and we spent an hour looking for the little silver shoe piece in the dark. I always played as the dog, anyway. Dogs are just better, you know? I really want to get a golden retriever someday, but my apartment complex has a strict no-pets policy. They don't even allow fish tanks over ten gallons because of \"water damage liability,\" which seems a little extreme. But no, no excessive worry. Just this massive weight of not having the energy to deal with any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric for a long time?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. When I first started this new assignment at the plant, I felt... I don't know, almost electrically upbeat? Like I was just vibrating with all these incredible ideas for streamlining the production line. But then I got really, weirdly irritable because the floor supervisors kept questioning my plans, and it felt like they were just slowing down something that was so obviously important and brilliant. Which is exactly what happened when I tried to reorganize my kitchen cabinets last year. I had this whole system figured out based on frequency of use and ergonomic reach, but my sister came over and said it didn't make sense to put the coffee mugs on the bottom shelf. But she doesn't even drink coffee! She's one of those people who drinks matcha lattes every morning. She buys this ceremonial grade powder online that costs like forty dollars a tin. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. But yeah, at the beginning of the assignment, I just felt super charged up and then really annoyed with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric that lasted for at least several days?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this intense shift from being in total overdrive to barely functioning at all, it's been going on since I moved into that temporary apartment. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. It's one of those old electric coils that sits crooked, so the pan slides to one side and all the oil pools at the edge. It makes cooking chicken practically impossible because one side burns and the other is raw. But anyway, yeah, this whole up and down thing has been happening for about three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods like this in the past where you felt extremely upbeat and then crashed, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I mean, I've had times where I felt pretty good and then kind of tired, but I don't know if it's like this. It's hard to remember, especially because my memory has felt so fuzzy lately. It's like trying to remember a dream right after you wake up, you know? You have the feeling of it, but the details just slip away. I had this dream the other night that I was trying to run on a treadmill, but the belt was made of quicksand, and I just kept sinking. I woke up sweating, and it was freezing in that apartment because the windows are super drafty. I bought this plastic film stuff you're supposed to shrink-wrap over the windows with a hair dryer to keep the cold out, but my hair dryer broke. It just sparked and died right in the middle of drying my hair before work last Tuesday. I had to go in with half-wet hair, and it was so embarrassing. My hair always frizzes up when it air dries. I used to use this special anti-frizz serum, but they discontinued it. Why do companies always discontinue the best products? It's like they want us to be unhappy. So, I don't know if I've had periods exactly like this before. It's just hard to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these extreme mood shifts from being very upbeat to feeling completely drained before in your life?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I really can't say for sure. It's just hard to pin down if I've had these exact same shifts before. I mean, my mood definitely fluctuates, but who doesn't have good days and bad days? It's just that right now everything feels so magnified, probably because of the stress of the new assignment and the move. Moving is just so disruptive to your routine. I usually go to this spin class on Tuesdays and Thursdays, but the studio is all the way across town now. The instructor, Dave, plays the best 80s rock playlists. I tried to find a new studio closer to the temporary apartment, but they all play that awful EDM music with the heavy bass that gives me a headache. I get migraines sometimes, especially when the barometric pressure drops. My mom gets them too. She swears by peppermint oil rubbed on her temples, but I think it just makes you smell like a candy cane. Candy canes always remind me of when I was a kid and we'd decorate the tree on Christmas Eve. We had these vintage glass ornaments that were so fragile. Anyway, no, I just really don't know if I've had these extreme shifts like this before. It's all just a blur right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Like I just said, I... I really don't know. I can't remember another time when I felt like that for days on end. It's just so hard to think back clearly when my head feels like it's stuffed with cotton right now. Cotton always makes me think of those cheap pillows they give you in hotels. You know the ones? They look all fluffy until you lay your head on them, and then they just flatten into a pancake. I travel a lot for work, inspecting different production facilities, so I've slept on my fair share of terrible hotel pillows. There was this one place in Ohio, right near the stamping plant, where the pillow was actually lumpy. Like, literal chunks of foam inside. I tried folding it in half, but that just made it worse. The plant manager there was this guy named Gary who collected vintage lunchboxes. He had a whole wall of them in his office. A Six Million Dollar Man one, a Scooby-Doo one... it was actually kind of neat, but he talked about them for an hour while we were supposed to be reviewing safety protocols. Safety is a huge deal on the production floor. You have to wear steel-toed boots, which are so heavy. But yeah, I don't know if I've had another time where I was super energetic like that. I just can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic, followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I really just don't have a better answer for you. I haven't kept a log of my moods or anything, so I honestly don't know if there's been a pattern of going up and then crashing. It's kind of like asking me to pull a specific quality assurance report from, I don't know, three years ago. Without a tracking number, it's just lost in the void. We actually used to use these massive, heavy physical binders for QA on the assembly line before we finally switched to digital tablets last quarter. The tablets are supposed to be so much more efficient, but the glare on the screens under those harsh fluorescent factory lights is absolutely terrible. I had to order these special matte anti-glare screen protectors, but the purchasing department held up the order for three weeks because of some ridiculous budget freeze. Purchasing departments always ruin everything, I swear. They'll pinch pennies on a fifty-dollar pack of screen protectors, but then they turn around and approve a ten-thousand-dollar catering budget for the regional executive retreat. It makes absolutely no sense, the way corporate allocates funds. But anyway, yeah, I haven't noticed a specific timeline of episodes like what you're asking about. I just really couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, I know you keep asking that, and I guess... I mean, at the very beginning of this new assignment, I did feel almost electrically upbeat for a while. Like I was totally dialed in. But then it turned into me getting weirdly irritable. It just felt like anyone who was questioning my ideas was slowing down something that was so obvious and important, you know? Like Greg from engineering. He always has to play devil's advocate on the floor. And he wears these awful argyle sweater vests, even in the summer. Who wears wool in July? The AC in the main office isn't even that great anyway because the HVAC system hasn't been updated since 1998. They keep saying they're going to overhaul it, but then they just bring in these cheap little oscillating fans that just push the hot air around the room. It dries out my contacts so badly. I actually had to switch to dailies because my eyes were getting so red and dry. Dailies are so ridiculously expensive though, and my vision insurance only covers a fraction of the cost. Vision insurance is basically a scam if you ask me. But yeah, I was definitely feeling really upbeat and then just so annoyed with everyone at the start of this project.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer, before this recent experience at the plant?\n\nPatient: Well, before this plant assignment... I guess there was a time, yeah. For a stretch, I had this massive surplus of energy. It was like I didn't even need to sleep. I remember I unpacked all my boxes after midnight, completely reorganized my apartment, answered a mountain of work emails, and I still wanted to drive back to the plant to check on one more thing on the line. Driving at night is actually really peaceful, though. There's hardly anyone on the road, especially on that stretch of highway near my place. They've been doing construction on it for what feels like three years. They always leave those bright orange barrels out, even when no one is working. I don't understand why they can't just move them to the shoulder. It creates this artificial bottleneck that backs up traffic for miles during rush hour. And people are so aggressive when they have to merge. This one guy in a silver pickup truck almost ran me off the road last week because he refused to zipper merge properly. Zipper merging is the most efficient way to handle a lane closure, it's mathematically proven, but people just get so territorial about their spot in line. It's like they think they're losing some sort of race. But yeah, I definitely had that period where I was just going and going, unpacking and working all night.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Although, I did see on the news that they're predicting a really bad hurricane season this year. The ocean temperatures are apparently way higher than average, which fuels the storms. I remember when Hurricane Sandy hit, I was in college, and we lost power for like five days. We ended up playing Monopoly by candlelight, which sounds fun, but Monopoly actually takes forever and always ends in an argument. My roommate got so mad she flipped the board, and we spent an hour looking for the little silver shoe piece in the dark. I always played as the dog, anyway. Dogs are just better, you know? I really want to get a golden retriever someday, but my apartment complex has a strict no-pets policy. They don't even allow fish tanks over ten gallons because of \"water damage liability,\" which seems a little extreme. But no, no excessive worry. I had this whole system figured out based on frequency of use and ergonomic reach, but my sister came over and said it didn't make sense to put the coffee mugs on the bottom shelf. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. I really need to take it to a mechanic, but finding a mechanic you can trust is impossible. They always try to upsell you on air filters. But yeah, at the beginning of the assignment, I just felt super charged up and then really annoyed with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or euphoric that lasted for at least several days?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this intense shift from being in total overdrive to barely functioning at all, it's been going on since I moved into that temporary apartment. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. Oh my gosh, they make these incredible almond croissants. The baker actually studied in Paris, and you can totally tell because the pastry is so flaky, not like that dense stuff you get at the grocery store. I don't understand why they can't just finish paving one lane before they tear up the next one. I tried it once, and it honestly just tastes like grass clippings. Reminds me of when I used to mow the lawn for my parents in high school to earn extra money for gas. Gas prices were so much cheaper back then. Now it costs me fifty bucks just to fill up my Honda, and the check engine light has been on for three weeks. Which was about three weeks ago. Moving is just the worst, isn't it? I had to pack up all my stuff in these cardboard boxes I got from the grocery store, and half of them smelled like bananas. I don't even like bananas. The texture is just too mushy, unless they're baked into bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread with walnuts, but she'd always burn the edges just a little bit. She said the burnt parts gave it character. She was full of sayings like that. She used to tell me that a watched pot never boils, which is scientifically inaccurate, but I guess I get the point. I tried to make pasta the other night in the apartment, and the stove took forever to heat up. It's one of those old electric coils that sits crooked, so the pan slides to one side and all the oil pools at the edge. But anyway, yeah, this whole up and down thing has been happening for about three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods like this in the past where you felt extremely upbeat and then crashed, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I mean, I've had times where I felt pretty good and then kind of tired, but I don't know if it's like this. It's hard to remember, especially because my memory has felt so fuzzy lately. I had this dream the other night that I was trying to run on a treadmill, but the belt was made of quicksand, and I just kept sinking. It's all just a blur right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Like I just said, I... I really don't know. I can't remember another time when I felt like that for days on end. It's just so hard to think back clearly when my head feels like it's stuffed with cotton right now. Cotton always makes me think of those cheap pillows they give you in hotels. You know the ones? They look all fluffy until you lay your head on them, and then they just flatten into a pancake. I travel a lot for work, inspecting different production facilities, so I've slept on my fair share of terrible hotel pillows. There was this one place in Ohio, right near the stamping plant, where the pillow was actually lumpy. Like, literal chunks of foam inside. I tried folding it in half, but that just made it worse. The plant manager there was this guy named Gary who collected vintage lunchboxes. He had a whole wall of them in his office. A Six Million Dollar Man one, a Scooby-Doo one... it was actually kind of neat, but he talked about them for an hour while we were supposed to be reviewing safety protocols. Safety is a huge deal on the production floor. You have to wear steel-toed boots, which are so heavy. But yeah, I don't know if I've had another time where I was super energetic like that. I just can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic, followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I really just don't have a better answer for you. I haven't kept a log of my moods or anything, so I honestly don't know if there's been a pattern of going up and then crashing. It's kind of like asking me to pull a specific quality assurance report from, I don't know, three years ago. Without a tracking number, it's just lost in the void. We actually used to use these massive, heavy physical binders for QA on the assembly line before we finally switched to digital tablets last quarter. The tablets are supposed to be so much more efficient, but the glare on the screens under those harsh fluorescent factory lights is absolutely terrible. I had to order these special matte anti-glare screen protectors, but the purchasing department held up the order for three weeks because of some ridiculous budget freeze. Purchasing departments always ruin everything, I swear. They'll pinch pennies on a fifty-dollar pack of screen protectors, but then they turn around and approve a ten-thousand-dollar catering budget for the regional executive retreat. It makes absolutely no sense, the way corporate allocates funds. But anyway, yeah, I haven't noticed a specific timeline of episodes like what you're asking about. I just really couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life when you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, I know you keep asking that, and I guess... I mean, at the very beginning of this new assignment, I did feel almost electrically upbeat for a while. Like I was totally dialed in. But then it turned into me getting weirdly irritable. It just felt like anyone who was questioning my ideas was slowing down something that was so obvious and important, you know? Like Greg from engineering. He always has to play devil's advocate on the floor. And he wears these awful argyle sweater vests, even in the summer. Who wears wool in July? The AC in the main office isn't even that great anyway because the HVAC system hasn't been updated since 1998. I actually had to switch to dailies because my eyes were getting so red and dry. Dailies are so ridiculously expensive though, and my vision insurance only covers a fraction of the cost. Vision insurance is basically a scam if you ask me. But yeah, I was definitely feeling really upbeat and then just so annoyed with everyone at the start of this project.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another time in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer, before this recent experience at the plant?\n\nPatient: Well, before this plant assignment... I guess there was a time, yeah. For a stretch, I had this massive surplus of energy. It was like I didn't even need to sleep. I remember I unpacked all my boxes after midnight, completely reorganized my apartment, answered a mountain of work emails, and I still wanted to drive back to the plant to check on one more thing on the line. Driving at night is actually really peaceful, though. There's hardly anyone on the road, especially on that stretch of highway near my place. They've been doing construction on it for what feels like three years. They always leave those bright orange barrels out, even when no one is working.
