It's not like I keep a spreadsheet of my feelings, though honestly, a spreadsheet would probably be more organized than my brain right now. I use spreadsheets for literally everything at work. Excel is just so much better than whatever proprietary software the company tries to force on us. They rolled out this new inventory management system last year, and it was a complete disaster. The interface was so clunky, it looked like it was designed in the early 2000s. And the training they gave us? A joke. It was just a two-hour webinar with this guy who kept clearing his throat directly into the microphone. It was so distracting. I had to mute it halfway through and just read the manual myself. Manuals are usually terribly written anyway, always translated poorly from another language. But yeah, I don't really know if I've had these shifts more than once. I just haven't been keeping track.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic for several days or longer, separate from the one you described at the beginning of your plant assignment?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. I mean, besides that time I just told you about, when I was unpacking all those boxes... I don't really remember any other time like that. It's so hard to remember things clearly anyway. My memory has been terrible lately, which is probably because I've been trying to learn Spanish on this app on my phone. It's supposed to just take fifteen minutes a day, but I end up spending an hour trying to get the pronunciation right. The owl character they use is so passive-aggressive if you miss a day, too. It sends these notifications that make you feel guilty, like, \"These reminders don't seem to be working. We'll stop sending them for now.\" It's just a cartoon bird, but it actually makes me feel bad. I always thought birds were kind of creepy anyway. Their eyes are so beady and they just stare at you. There was this pigeon outside my window the other day that just sat there for hours. Just staring. It was unnerving. But no, I can't think of any other time I felt like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these extreme mood shifts from being very upbeat to feeling completely drained more than once in your life?\n\nPatient: I don't think so... I mean, going from totally wired to completely dead? Not that I can recall. Like I said, I really only remember that one time with the boxes and the emails. It's not like my life is this constant rollercoaster. Though, speaking of rollercoasters, I used to love them when I was a kid. We'd go to Cedar Point every summer, and I'd ride the Millennium Force like five times in a row. Now, just thinking about going upside down makes me nauseous. It's weird how your body changes as you get older, you know? Like, I can't even eat spicy food anymore without getting terrible heartburn. I had this amazing Thai green curry last week—or tried to—and I was up half the night paying for it. Which is a shame because Thai food is my absolute favorite. The place near my apartment makes this incredible Pad See Ew, but their delivery guy always gets lost. He calls me from, like, three streets over every single time. I don't get how hard it is to use a GPS. It's literally a computer in your pocket that tells you exactly where to go. But yeah, no, I haven't really had that up-and-down thing happen more than once.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these extreme mood shifts from being very upbeat to feeling completely drained before this recent episode at the plant?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. I mean, I feel like I would remember if I had crashed that hard before. It was just... it was so intense, you know? Like hitting a brick wall. But no, before this whole thing at the plant, I can't think of it ever happening. The plant has been so stressful anyway. We've been trying to meet these ridiculous production quotas because upper management decided to promise a new client a turnaround time that's physically impossible with our current machinery. I keep telling them we need to upgrade the conveyor belts, but they won't approve the budget. It's always about the budget with them. Meanwhile, I'm the one on the floor trying to explain to the operators why they have to work mandatory overtime again. And the break room doesn't even have a working microwave right now. Someone put tinfoil in it last Tuesday and it sparked and died. You'd think adults would know not to put metal in a microwave, but apparently not. I ended up having to eat my soup cold, which is just sad. Cold minestrone is basically just wet salad. But yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't had those shifts before this recent stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic, followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so... I mean, I feel like you've asked me this a few times now, and I keep trying to rack my brain, but I just don't remember it happening more than this one time. It's just the one time with the boxes and the crash after. I really don't think it's a pattern. Speaking of patterns, though, I've been trying to learn how to knit. I thought it would be relaxing, you know? Like something to do with my hands while I watch TV. I bought all this expensive yarn online—merino wool, really soft—and these bamboo needles. But the patterns are so complicated. It's like reading a foreign language. \"Knit two together, purl one, yarn over.\" I tried making a scarf, but the edges started curling up, and it ended up looking like a weird, fuzzy snake. My cat loved it, though. He dragged it under the sofa and now it's covered in dust bunnies. He’s always doing that, hiding things. I found three of my missing socks under the fridge yesterday. How does a cat even get a sock under a fridge? But no, to answer you, I haven't had more than one of those up-and-down episodes. Just the one.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced more than one episode in your life where you felt extremely upbeat or energetic, followed by a crash, before the one that started with your plant redesign projects?\n\nPatient: Honestly, doc, I feel like we're going in circles here. I really don't think I've had anything like that before the whole plant redesign thing. It was just that one time. I mean, my memory isn't perfect, but I'm pretty sure I'd remember crashing that hard. Speaking of memory, though, I've been trying to improve mine by doing those crossword puzzles in the Sunday paper. My grandma used to do them in pen, which I always thought was so arrogant, you know? Like, what if you make a mistake? I use a pencil, but even then, the erasers on those cheap mechanical pencils just smudge the graphite everywhere, and then you can't even read the clues underneath. I tried switching to Sudoku, but staring at all those numbers just gives me a headache. It's like looking at the spreadsheets for the plant's quarterly projections, which I'm already way behind on. My boss keeps emailing me about them, and I just keep flagging them and ignoring them. But anyway, no, I haven't had any other episodes like that before the plant stuff. Just the one."
